# Elite Knight hunting guide by comfort (Tibia, Nov 2026)

This guide is for anyone playing an Elite Knight alone, including players who are brand new to Tibia. It answers one question at every level: **where can I hunt without stress, and still make good experience and gold?**

Everything here describes Tibia as it is in November 2026, after the big vocation changes of June 2026. Older guides and videos may give different advice, because spells, damage and potions changed a lot.

## How to use this guide
The game has many systems that make a knight stronger: imbuements, charms, the Wheel of Destiny and more. Each one helps, but each one is also something new to learn, buy or keep track of. This guide adds them **one at a time**, in sections:

- **Section 1, Basic mechanics:** just your weapon, one attack spell, one healing spell and potions. Full hunting ground lists.
- **Section 1a, Stealth Ring:** a ring that hides you from many monsters. Hunting ground lists.
- **Section 1b, Hunting grounds behind quests:** good hunting grounds that open only after part of a quest, and the quests to do. Hunting ground lists.
- **Section 1c, Hunting grounds with a long trip:** good hunting grounds more than about 2 minutes from the depot. Hunting ground lists.
- **Section 2, Imbuements:** life and mana leech on your gear. Full hunting ground lists.
- **Section 3, Charms and prey:** bonuses against the monsters you hunt. Hunting ground lists.
- **Section 4, Wheel of Destiny and gems:** permanent perks from your level. Hunting ground lists.
- **Sections 5 and 6, Weapon proficiency and the Forge:** the last steps to a full setup, explained with numbers.

Start in Section 1. Find your level in the hunting ground tables and pick a hunting ground from its **Good for XP** list to level up, or from its **Good for gold** list when you need money for gear, supplies or your promotion. When hunting there feels easy, move on to the next section and see which new hunting grounds open up.

From level 100, [Comfortable solo hunting at 100+](comfort_solo_100.md) is a calmer way to play: auto attack and Shield Bash with small pulls, two cheap leech imbuements doing most of the healing, and the best hunting grounds per band up to level 800+.

If you test a hunting ground, write down how it went in `spot_tests.md`. Tested hunting grounds are marked with ✓ and use real numbers instead of estimates.

## Words you'll see
- **HP and mana:** your health and the energy you spend on spells. A knight has about 185 + 15 × (level − 8) HP: 815 at level 50, 2,075 at level 134.
- **Spawn:** the monsters that live in a hunting ground and come back after you kill them.
- **Box, boxing:** letting several monsters surround you and hitting them all at once with an area spell. Bigger boxes kill faster but hurt more.
- **`exori infir min`:** a weak attack spell every knight has from level 1. It hits up to three monsters in front of you for only 6 mana.
- **`exori ico scu` (Shield Bash):** a single-target attack (level 18, premium, 30 mana, every 4 seconds) whose damage comes from your shield's defense. It also halves the target's next attack. You need a shield to cast it.
- **`exori`:** the knight's basic area attack (level 35, premium account). It hits every monster standing next to you.
- **`exura ico`:** the knight's basic healing spell.
- **XP/h, loot/h:** experience and gold (the value of what monsters drop) per hour of hunting.
- **Depot:** your storage in each city. It's also where the Market is.
- **Market:** where players buy and sell items. Many monster drops sell there for far more than shops pay.
- **Band:** a level range in the hunting ground tables, such as 40–60: 20 levels apart up to 100, then 50.

## Comfort: the idea behind this guide
Some hunting grounds ask almost nothing of you: you walk in, attack, and never need to heal. Others ask a lot: you have to watch your health all the time, carry many potions, avoid certain rooms, or bring special gear. This guide calls that difference **comfort**.

Comfort depends on your level. A hunting ground that is dangerous at level 50 can be relaxing at level 130, because you have more HP and hit harder.

Every hunting ground gets two grades from **0 (most comfortable) to 3 (least comfortable)**:
- **Hunt:** how much attention the hunt itself needs.
- **Prep:** how much you need to bring or buy before you go.

| Grade | Hunt (during the hunt) | Prep (before the hunt) |
|---|---|---|
| **0** | You just attack. No healing needed. | Nothing special. |
| **1** | You heal now and then with `exura ico`. | A little healing, all from your own mana. |
| **2** | You box monsters and heal often. | A lot of healing, or a specific piece of gear. |
| **3** | You need special tactics: invisible monsters, monsters that call helpers, rooms to avoid. | Something from a later section of this guide. |

The best hunting grounds are **Prep 0, Hunt 0** ("P0 H0" in short). That's the dream: no healing, no worries, just hunting. Every listed hunting ground already needs no potions.

There's a third kind of comfort, **knowledge**: how much of the game you need to understand. That's what the sections are for. Each section has a **kit**, the things everyone in that section uses. In Section 2, for example, everyone has the two leech imbuements, so they don't count against a hunting ground's Prep grade.

### How a hunting ground is graded
For hunting grounds that were tested, the grades come from what actually happened: how much healing was needed and how much was spent on potions.

For the others, the grade comes from the fight-by-fight model in the appendix. It checks two things for the band's typical knight:
- **How much you need to heal:** the HP the monsters take from you in an hour, after your armor, shield and protections, counted in full HP bars.
- **The worst moment:** the hardest round of a typical pull, every monster in it hitting its hardest at once, as a share of your HP.

| Healing per hour | Worst round | Hunt and Prep |
|---|---|---|
| less than 3 HP bars | less than 30% of your HP | 0 |
| less than 15 | less than 50% | 1 |
| less than 40 | less than 80% | 2 |
| more | more | 3 |

The grade is the first row where both numbers fit. For example, a hunting ground where you heal 10 full HP bars an hour and the worst round takes 40% of your HP is Hunt 1.

A few things still raise it:
- **Invisible monsters.** You can't see them to attack them, so the hunting ground is Hunt 3.
- **A rare monster that can take half your HP in one turn** makes the hunting ground at least Hunt 2.
- **Special gear.** If the hunting ground needs a specific item, like an elemental armor or a Dwarven Ring, Prep is 2.

Monsters that call helpers are part of the model: the helpers' damage counts in your healing.

Some hunting grounds are left out entirely: those where most monsters (40% or more) call several helpers at once or call helpers and slow you, like the Bonelord caves, or with many monsters that hurt you but give no experience. Where one in five or more does it, the hunting ground stays in the lists with Hunt one step higher.

### Which hunting grounds are listed
A hunting ground shows up only from the level TibiaWiki suggests for it (the Brazilian TibiaWiki when it has the page), then in every band where our model rates it comfortable, so the same hunting ground often appears in several bands, and its grades and numbers get better as your level goes up. The one exception is a hunting ground a test showed is fine earlier: the notes say so.

Each band shows two short lists: **Good for XP**, the 3 hunting grounds with the most experience per hour, and **Good for gold**, the 3 with the most loot per hour. Only hunting grounds you can hunt **without any potions** are listed: see How to fight. Both lists take only comfortable hunting grounds (Prep and Hunt of 0 or 1), and allow a 2 only when fewer than 3 qualify. A hunting ground can be in both lists. The later sections show a band only when their system changes these lists. Hunting grounds that need a quest first are left out of every section except Section 1b (the Targuna Quest and starting The New Frontier Quest don't count), and hunting grounds more than about 2 minutes from the depot are only in Section 1c. The tables start at level 20, and Sections 2 to 4 at level 50.

### The character the numbers assume
The XP and loot numbers are estimates for a typical knight: a new character following this guide, hunting 3 hours a day and training offline 12 hours a day (counting only the offline training, to be safe). That gives skill 43 at level 20, 63 at 50, 81 at 100, 97 at 200, 100 at 300 and 105 at 500. From level 200 every section also assumes Basic Vampirism plus a Basic Void on both the helmet and the weapon (about 6.5k gold an hour) and the Wheel's extra HP (Section 4): a knight that level always has them. They wear the best gear they could buy with about 3 hours of hunting income, and each band lists that gear. If your skill or gear is better, you'll do better than the tables. Playing more hours a day means a lower skill for your level.

## Section 1: Basic mechanics
In this section you use only the basics: your weapon, the `exori infir min`, `exori ico scu` and `exori` attack spells, the `exura ico` healing spell, potions, normal gear, and an amulet and ring that never run out. Everything else comes later.

### How to fight
**Before level 35**, fight one or two monsters at a time with normal attacks, and cast `exori infir min` (6 mana, hits up to three monsters in front of you) right after an attack whenever it's ready. From level 18, cast `exori ico scu` (Shield Bash, 30 mana, every 4 seconds) on your target instead: it hits harder and halves the target's next attack. Heal with `exura ico`.

**From level 35** you learn `exori` (premium, 125 mana), which hits every monster next to you. Let two or three monsters surround you (**boxing**), cast `exori` whenever it's ready and attack normally in between. Add one more monster at a time while your HP stays above about 60%. Against one or two monsters, normal attacks and `exori ico scu` are enough.

**The tables assume no potions at all.** Cast `exori`, `exori gran` and `exori ico scu` only as often as your mana lasts without drinking, and keep enough to heal with `exura ico` first. Mana comes back slowly on its own (also while you walk and loot) and, from Section 2, with the Void imbuement, so more leech means more spells and faster hunts. The numbers are fitted to a real level 99 hunt with Basic leech and no potions. **From level 90** add `exori gran` (360 mana, about twice the damage of `exori`) once on each box of three or more, as the tables do.

**Let each normal attack land.** Normal attacks do a good share of your damage and also trigger leech, critical hits, charms and tier effects, so cast your spells right after an attack lands, not over it, and you won't lose attack turns. Turning the game sound on, with only your own attacks and the monsters' hits, makes the rhythm easy to follow. Keep your target on the monster with the most health, so the group dies together.

**Potions** share one short cooldown (about 1 second). The tables count none, but keep a few: a health potion for when your HP drops fast, and mana potions if you want more spells than your mana allows (faster, but no longer potion-free). Stronger potions unlock at levels 50, 80, 130 and 200. Since June 2026 knights can use Great Mana Potions too (level 80, about 200 mana for 158 gold): they need fewer potion turns in the harder bands, though with the leech imbuements of Section 2 you'll rarely need many potions.

**Multi-action buttons:** one action bar button can hold up to three spells (right-click it and choose Assign Multiaction). Each press casts the first one that isn't on cooldown, so pressing again while the first is cooling down sends the next. Attack spells share a 2-second cooldown, so the next press works 2 seconds later. A good setup on keys 2 to 5, each spell from the level shown:
- **2, healing:** heal spells are ready again after 1 second, before the shared cooldown ends, so the first slot always fires. Keeping `exura infir ico`, `exura ico` and `exura med ico` on one button still keeps them in one place: move the one a hunting ground needs to the first slot. Keep `exura gran ico` (once every 2 minutes) on its own button for emergencies.
- **3, single target:** `exori ico scu` (level 18), then `exori ico` (16), then `exori hur` (28). One press is Shield Bash; press again only when you want more damage. Shield Bash goes first because it's the strongest and comes back every 4 seconds, so it's cast more often.
- **4, small box (3 or 4 monsters):** `exori ico scu` (level 18), then `exori min` (Front Sweep, 70), then `exori` (35); before level 70, `exori infir min` (level 1) goes in the middle slot. From level 35, pressing every 2 seconds casts Shield Bash, the sweep, Shield Bash, `exori`, and round again, with no empty press. The sweep hits only the three tiles in front of you, so turn on the client's Aim at Target option for it: it turns you to your target before casting. On three monsters `exori min` (200 mana) costs less mana for its damage than `exori` or `exori gran`, which counts when you hunt without potions; `exori` only does better from four monsters (five with the Wheel's Front Sweep perk). Against one or two monsters use the single-target button: there the sweep and `exori` waste mana. The tables don't count this button.
- **5, big box (5 or more):** `exori scu` (Shield Slam, level 30), then `exori` (35), then `exori gran` (90). The first press is the cheapest; the 360-mana `exori gran` fires on the third press in a row, once on each box.

**Stay safe:** always know your way out, and leave when your HP falls to about half.

### Gear
In Section 1 you wear only items that never run out, so there are no charges or timers to watch. Each band's gear line lists good-value weapons, armor, amulet and ring. A piece with physical protection (like the Zaoan helmet and legs) counts it on top of its armor: 5% less physical damage is worth more than a few armor points once monsters hit hard.
- **Amulet and ring:** a Platinum Amulet, then a Foxtail Amulet from level 100 (5% less physical damage), and a Butterfly Ring from level 50.
- **Elemental hunting grounds:** where one element does most of the damage, an elemental armor (Lavos, Crystalline, Swamplair or Voltage, level 60) helps; the notes say when.

### Before you hunt
- **Stamina:** with a premium account, the first 3 hours from full stamina (42 hours) give 50% more experience, so start your best hunts after a long rest. Below 14 hours you get half the experience and no loot. Stamina comes back while you're logged out, but only after 10 minutes offline, so each quick login between hunts (to check the Market, for example) costs up to 10 minutes of refill, almost 2 minutes of green stamina.
- **Blessings:** the 5 regular blessings, bought from NPCs in the cities, make you lose about 70% less experience and skill when you die, and you keep your items. They cost about (level − 20) × 1,000 gold up to level 120. They're worth it: where and how in [Blessings, step by step](#blessings-step-by-step).
- **Food:** eat until the game says you're full before the hunt, and again now and then; food makes you regenerate.
- **Daily reward:** take it every day at the Reward Wall (potions or runes, Prey Wildcards, training weapons, an XP boost). The streak also makes you regenerate faster in temples, depots and houses.
- **Buy potions on the Market first:** potions handed out as daily rewards often sell there far below NPC prices.

### Blessings, step by step
Blessings cut what you lose when you die. With the 5 regular ones you keep all your items, and a promoted character loses 70% less experience and skill; the 2 enhanced ones take that to 86%. What counts is how many you have, not which: any 5 keep all your items, while with 4 each piece still has a 1% chance to drop and your backpack 10%. The 6th and 7th only cut the experience and skill loss further, so the 2 enhanced ones, the dearest and farthest, are the ones to skip when gold or time is short. A death takes them all away, so buy them again after each one. **Twist of Fate**, sold by any temple NPC, only keeps the others safe when another player kills you.

At each NPC say **hi**, then the blessing's name (for example **spiritual shielding**), then **yes** to pay. Kais and the Nomad also answer to **enhanced**.

| Blessing | NPC | Where | Good to know |
|---|---|---|---|
| Spiritual Shielding | Norf | the White Flower Temple, south of Thais ([map](https://tibiamaps.io/map#32346,32364,7:2)) | |
| Wisdom of Solitude | Eremo | Eremo's Island, by boat from Cormaya ([map](https://tibiamaps.io/map#33322,31884,7:2)) | |
| Spark of the Phoenix | Kawill, then Pydar | Kazordoon: Kawill south-west of the temple, one floor down ([map](https://tibiamaps.io/map#32644,31969,12:2)); Pydar north of the temple, past some fire fields ([map](https://tibiamaps.io/map#32655,31893,11:2)) | talk to Kawill first; you pay only Pydar, 10% less with a Phoenix Egg in your backpack if you finished [The Paradox Tower Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/The_Paradox_Tower_Quest) |
| Fire of the Suns | Edala | the Suntower, south of Ab'Dendriel ([map](https://tibiamaps.io/map#32697,31719,7:2)) | |
| Embrace of Tibia | Humphrey | the small temple north of Carlin ([map](https://tibiamaps.io/map#32358,31688,7:2)) | |
| Blood of the Mountain (enhanced) | Kais | northern Tiquanda, down a hole ([map](https://tibiamaps.io/map#32910,32515,7:2)) | bring a Shovel; apes (Kongra, Sibang) and terror birds on the way from Port Hope (TibiaWiki suggests level 50 for Banuta), and giant spiders one floor below the area |
| Heart of the Mountain (enhanced) | Nomad | ask Buddel in Svargrond for a passage to the Raider Camp, south of Bittermor on Hrodmir, then use Levitate ([map](https://tibiamaps.io/map#31940,31307,7:2)) | Buddel's boat needs the [Barbarian Test Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Barbarian_Test_Quest); an easy walk from the camp (mammoths, winter wolves) |

**A route for the 5 regular ones** (TibiaWiki): Norf south of Thais; the boat to Edron, then to Cormaya and Eremo's Island; back to Edron and the Magic Carpet to Kazordoon (Kawill, then Pydar); the ore wagon to the north exit and a walk to Ab'Dendriel (Edala), then on to Carlin (Humphrey). Kazordoon's ore wagons need a 250-gold ticket from Lokur, the postman, or from Brodrosch at the steamship dock (the steamship from Cormaya is another way in); they link the entrance, the depot, the temple, the steamship and the shops. Kais and the Nomad are separate trips, to Tiquanda and to Svargrond.

**Cost** (TibiaWiki's formula: each regular blessing costs 200 × (level − 20) gold up to level 120, then 20,000 + 75 for each level above 120; each enhanced one about 30% more):

| Level | 5 regular | All 7 | All 7 + Twist of Fate |
|---|---|---|---|
| 30 | 10,000 | 15,200 | 17,200 |
| 50 | 30,000 | 45,600 | 51,600 |
| 100 | 80,000 | 121,600 | 137,600 |
| 150 | 111,250 | 169,250 | 195,250 |
| 200 | 130,000 | 198,000 | 234,000 |
| 300 | 167,500 | 255,500 | 305,500 |
| 500 | 242,500 | 370,500 | 420,500 |

The Store sells all 7 for 130 Tibia Coins, far more than the NPCs at any of these levels.

### Loot
- **Quick Loot:** Shift + right click on a corpse loots it. In the Options, under Hotkeys, put **Quick Loot Nearby Corpses** on a key next to your attack keys: it loots your square and the 8 around it at once.
- **Skip the junk:** right-click an item and choose "Add to Loot List". In Skipped Loot mode, Quick Loot takes everything except those items.
- **Sort it:** right-click a backpack and choose "Manage Containers" to send each category (gold, creature products, potions and more) to its own backpack. Put a few empty backpacks inside your loot backpack and you can loot much longer.
- **Sell on the Market:** many drops sell there for far more than shops pay (a Stone Wing sells for about 2.4k on the Market and 120 at a shop); the notes name each hunting ground's best drop. Selling to a buy offer is instant, but check that it beats the shop price. At the depot, "Stow container's content" puts stackable loot in your Supply Stash, and the Market sells straight from there.
- **Skinning:** use an **Obsidian Knife** (about 10–20k on the Market, or the [Obsidian Knife Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Obsidian_Knife_Quest)) on Dragon corpses: about 1 in 24 gives a **Green Dragon Leather** (about 15k on the Market, November 2026), around 670 gold per dragon on average, which nearly triples a dragon's loot. Dragon Lords give a Red Dragon Leather (about 2k; around 170 gold per kill) and Behemoths a Perfect Behemoth Fang (about 3k; around 300). Minotaurs, lizards and bonebeasts give almost nothing. The Scavenge minor charm (Section 3) raises the chance.
- **Count the gold you actually get.** The Hunt Analyser can be far off in either direction, and prices differ between worlds, sometimes by five times, so check your own Market before you pick a hunting ground for its loot. You can set your own price for any item in the analyser: give items that players want, like the ones for weekly delivery tasks, their Market price.

### Bonuses on top
The tables don't count any of these or the Task Board below, so they all come on top.
- **The boosted creature:** every day at server save, one creature starts giving twice the experience and loot, and respawns twice as fast. It's shown on the login screen. If it lives in a hunting ground for your level, hunt there that day.
- **Echo Raids:** now and then a creature leaves an echo behind. Setting it off brings 8 more of that creature, or 4 influenced ones (stronger, and they give Dust), for 10 minutes. About 1 time in 10 (1 in 5 once that creature's Bestiary entry is finished) an **Echo Warden** comes instead: a stronger version that also makes the creatures near it stronger. A Warden always drops a Basic imbuement scroll and a weapon proficiency catalyst, and the first Warden of each creature gives 1 to 30 charm points, more for tougher creatures. Stepping on the echo sets it off (so does using it), so on a hunting ground that already keeps you busy, walk around it.
- **Discovery:** the Cyclopedia map splits Tibia into areas, each with points of interest to find. Find all of them in an area and you get 100 to 195 extra speed there for good, which makes walking between pulls and getting away easier. It's worth doing around the hunting grounds you use most.
- **Animus Mastery:** fiendish monsters (rare, at most 4 on a world at a time; `exiva moe res` points the way to one) drop a Soul Core about 1 time in 6, and Soul Cores also sell on the Market. Use one at the Soulpit, north-east of Hrodmir, and survive the waves of that creature it starts: from then on, that creature gives 2% more experience (up to 4% as you unlock more creatures). The waves don't get easier when you go alone, and dying there costs the usual, so it's optional.
- **Loyalty:** each 360 days of premium time the account has used add 5% to your skills, a small boost (about one level at skill 100), up to 50%. It only comes with time.

### Task Board: bounty and weekly tasks
The Task Board pays at every level, from your first hunts.

**Buy a Bounty Talisman as soon as you have 5,000 gold.** Jewellers in the cities sell it, and it goes in the extra slot. Even with no upgrades it gives **2.5% more damage, 2.5% life leech and 2.5% more loot** against your current bounty creature, and a 5% chance that a kill counts twice in the Bestiary. It works only while you wear it, and only against that creature. Its upgrades belong to your character, not to the item.

**Bounty tasks:** choose a difficulty (the same setting picks your weekly kill tasks) and the board offers 3 creatures. Take one, kill that many (summons' kills don't count) and report the task for a little experience, Bounty Points and a Reroll Token, which swaps the 3 offers for new ones. Now and then a task comes as Silver (twice the experience and points) or Gold (four times).

| Difficulty | Creatures (Bestiary difficulty) | Kills | Bounty Points |
|---|---|---|---|
| Beginner | Easy | 50–100 | 3 |
| Adept | Easy and Medium | 100–200 | 7 |
| Expert | Medium and Hard | 200–400 | 16 |
| Master | Hard and Challenging | 300–600 | 27 |

- **Take a creature from the hunting ground you're going to anyway.** The task then finishes by itself and the talisman works the whole hunt. If none of the 3 offers fits, use a Reroll Token. The Bestiary shows each creature's difficulty: Cyclopes and Minotaurs are Easy, while Stonerefiners, Dragons and Wyverns are Medium, so they come only from Adept up.
- **Preferred creatures:** mark your hunting ground's main creature as preferred and it shows up more often. The first slot is free; the next ones cost 300, 600, 900 and 1,200 points.
- **For points, hunt where one creature makes up most of the kills.** Every kill there counts toward the task, so tasks finish fastest and the points come quickest. The notes in the hunting lists name each hunting ground's monsters, most common first.
- **Spend the points on the talisman.** A bonus's first upgrade costs 5 points and each next one 12 more (5, 17, 29, 41 and so on), for 0.5% each (1% for the Bestiary chance). Reaching 5% costs 145 points in total, and 10% costs 1,335. Past 10% each upgrade adds less: 0.25%, then 0.1% from 20%. Start with damage, which speeds up every kill, then life leech.

**Weekly tasks:** 6 kill and 6 delivery tasks every Monday, drawn for you. Each gives half a level of experience up to level 82, and 1,994 × your level after that (about 183k at level 92). One kill task is "Any Creature", 1,000 kills of anything: it completes itself wherever you hunt. Deliveries ask for items you can buy on the Market, so they turn gold into experience: each one saves about 20 minutes of hunting at level 50 and 50 minutes at level 90. On your main, buy them when you have gold to spare. On a character you plan to sell, buy only the cheap ones (the [0 to 100 guide](zero_to_100.md) shows the limit by level). Prices differ a lot between worlds: 20 Silver Poniards cost 74k on one and 256k on another.

### Train your skills while you're offline
Your sword (or club, or axe) skill decides how hard you hit, and at low levels it matters more than your level. Use a **Training Statue** (in every big city's training school, and on Targuna; later, a bed in your house), pick **Sword Fighting + Shielding** (or club or axe) and log out. Training starts after 10 minutes and runs up to 12 hours; the time comes back as you play. A new knight goes from about sword 16 and shielding 12 to sword 43 and shielding 36 in the first 12 hours, which more than doubles your damage. It's free, so do it every time you log out, from level 8.

**Faster, for gold:** an **exercise weapon** used on an exercise dummy (every training school has them) trains in 17 minutes what offline training does in about 4 hours. Each costs about 347k gold from magic shop and equipment NPCs, or 25 TC in the Store, so leave them for when gold comes easily. There are exercise swords, axes and clubs. An exercise shield does the same for shielding (about 8 hours' worth). The daily reward's training weapons are small free ones.

### Levels 8 to 20: Targuna
The tables start at level 20, because the first levels are best spent on **Targuna**, the island for levels 8 to 20: its quests are safe, its depot is a few seconds from its hunting grounds, and its monsters drop potions you'll use later. Train offline for 12 hours first, then do the three missions of the [Targuna Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Targuna_Quest). With the hunting you do along the way, they take you to about level 20.

| Mission | Who starts it | Level | What you do | Reward |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [An ancient enemy](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Targuna_Quest/Spoiler#An_ancient_enemy) | Lizzie | any | Kill the Lizard Commander two floors down in the Hidden Lizard Temple | 1,500 experience, 2 platinum coins |
| [A long lost treasure](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Targuna_Quest/Spoiler#A_long_lost_treasure) | Sterling | 10 | On Aragonia, kill 20 pirates and use 10 tortoise eggs; bring Morla's map to Sterling, then dig at the marked place (bring a rope and a shovel) | 3,000 experience and an Adventurer Backpack |
| [Burning Heat](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Targuna_Quest/Spoiler#Burning_Heat) | Emiliana | 12 | Go down through the Crimson Court and beat the Herald of Fire | 5,000 experience and the Reliable Ram mount (give the turnips to the ram) |

Tested by a knight at levels 19 to 23: Aragonia gave about 40k XP and 55k gold per hour with no supplies, Crimson Court about 45k XP, and the Wasp Dungeon near Thais about 70k gold. Targuna's hunting grounds are still in the 20–30 lists, so stay until level 20 to 25. Before you leave Newhaven, the starting island, do the [Newhaven Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Newhaven_Quest) (1,600 experience and a Guardian Ring) and the [Newhaven Legion Helmet Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Newhaven_Legion_Helmet_Quest); you can't go back. On the mainland, [The Scatterbrained Sorcerer Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/The_Scatterbrained_Sorcerer_Quest) (starts in Thais, 10,650 experience) is the best early one.

### Promotion at level 20
At **level 20**, with a premium account, buy your **promotion** for 20,000 gold to become an **Elite Knight**. The easiest place is King Tibianus III in Thais's castle; the rulers of Carlin, Kazordoon, Ankrahmun and Beregar and Rotem Valos at the Adventurers' Guild sell it too. Buy it as soon as you have the gold: you lose 30% less experience and skill when you die, regenerate faster, can learn promotion-only spells, and from level 51 get Wheel of Destiny points (Section 4). It pauses while your premium time is over.

### Spells worth learning later
The tables use only the basics, but a few spells make hunting calmer once you're used to them (all need premium):
- **Protector** (`utamo tempo`, from level 20): a stance that makes you take 15% less damage and deal 15% less. Turn it on when a group is bigger than you'd like.
- **`utura`** (level 50) and **`utura gran`** (level 100) heal you over a minute: cast one before each group. **`exura gran ico`** (level 80) is a big heal every 2 minutes, for emergencies.
- **`utani hur`** (level 14) makes you faster, and **`utani tempo hur`** (level 25) much faster for 5 seconds: your escape button.
- **Emergency items:** keep a **Stone Skin Amulet** (80% less physical and death damage for 5 hits) or a **Might Ring** (20% less damage for 20 hits) in your backpack, and put it on when a pull goes wrong. Each costs about 10–19k on the Market.

### Reading the hunting ground tables
- **Hunting ground:** the name links to the entrance on the tibiamaps.io map. ✓ means the hunting ground was tested inside that band (the notes give the level and the XP/h measured).
- **Prep and Hunt:** the comfort grades, 0 to 3. In Sections 1a to 4, "–" before the arrow means the hunting ground needs potions without that section.
- **XP/h and Loot/h:** estimated experience and gold per hour for the typical knight at the band's first and last level (~95k–185k), without bonuses and without potions. Each band lists the hunting grounds that suit its first level, then the ones that open later in the band (their notes start with "From level X"); the second number shows how much more they give as you level up. The notes of tested hunting grounds give the real results. Above level 200 no hunting ground has been tested yet: those numbers are the model's estimates, probably on the low side.
- **Depot:** the nearest depot, how many squares you walk from it to the entrance, and about how long that takes. For islands and hidden areas it lists each step: the walk to the boat (or teleport), the trip, and the walk from where you arrive. A short trip makes a hunting ground more comfortable: more of your time goes to hunting, refilling and selling are quick, and leaving when things go wrong is easy. A mount makes every trip a bit faster: it adds 10 speed, about 5–8% faster walking. The easiest one is the Reliable Ram from Targuna's Burning Heat quest; riding a mount needs a premium account.
- **Notes:** test results, how to get access, **Watch** (what makes the hunting ground less comfortable) and **Sells** (its most valuable drop).

Under the gear line, **Unlock first** lists the quests the band's hunting grounds need (do them once, and those hunting grounds open up), and **Worth doing** points out the rare quest whose experience is worth the trip.

Each band's title also shows roughly how long it takes to reach the next band: the experience between the two levels, divided level by level by the average XP/h of its **Good for XP** list at that level. It counts raw experience plus the low level bonus (every character below level 50 gets extra experience: almost double at level 8, about 60% more at level 20, and none from level 50 on), so the stamina bonus, XP boosts and prey make it faster.

## Section 1a: Stealth Ring
A **Stealth Ring** makes you invisible for 10 minutes. Many monsters can't see invisible players, so they simply ignore you. Players report that these monsters don't attack you or call helpers even while you're hitting them. You can kill them without taking any damage. Cyclops, forest furies, gloom wolves, amazons and barbarians are some of them. Stronger monsters, like dragons and dwarf guards, can see invisible players and attack you as usual.

The ring has its own section because it asks for more preparation: you have to buy rings and swap them every 10 minutes. In return, several better-paying hunting grounds become comfortable much earlier, especially between levels 20 and 90. You can use it on top of any later section.

### Using it
1. **Buy rings on the Market.** They cost about 800–1,500 gold each, depending on the world (around 1,000 is typical). You use 6 per hour, so about 6k gold per hour. The NPC Haroun in Ankrahmun sells them for 5,000 each, which is much more expensive.
2. **Put one on before you walk into the spawn**, and keep the next one ready on a hotkey. Swap when the current ring is almost used up. If it runs out in the middle of a group, they all attack you at once.
3. **Walk to the monsters.** Monsters that can't see you won't follow you, so you can't gather a group. Walk up to them and attack. `exori` still hits all the monsters next to you.
4. **Try one monster first** the first time, to be sure it really ignores you.
5. You can't log out right away while these monsters are on your screen, even with the ring on.

### What it changes
- **Levels 20 to 60:** more hunting grounds become potion-free and Prep 0, Hunt 0, among them the Corym Mines Stonerefiners from about level 30 (around 70k–130k XP an hour) and Forest Fury Camp from 40 instead of 90 (about 45k XP and 90k loot an hour at 40).
- **Levels 40 to 90:** the Lair of the Treeling Witch becomes comfortable from level 40 instead of about 180, and the Ragnir barbarians from 40.
- **Above level 100** little else changes: most monsters worth hunting there can see invisible players.

### Easy quests with the ring
Several old item quests are guarded mostly by monsters that can't see you, so the ring turns them into a walk. They give gear, not experience, but the gear is useful early on:

| Quest | Level | Reward | Still sees you |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Mintwallin Cyclops Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Mintwallin_Cyclops_Quest) | 25+ | Chain Armor, Chain Helmet, a Small Diamond | Minotaur Mages |
| [Noble Armor Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Noble_Armor_Quest) | 35+ | Noble Armor and Crown Helmet | Ghouls |
| [Giant Smithhammer Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Giant_Smithhammer_Quest) | 40 | Giant Smithhammer and Talon | Minotaur Mages |
| [Geomancer Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Geomancer_Quest) | 45+ | Dwarven Ring and two gems | the Dwarf Geomancer at the end |
| [Orc Fortress Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Orc_Fortress_Quest) | 50+ | Knight Armor, Knight Axe and Fire Sword | Orc Shamans, Orc Warlords, Orc Leaders and a Dragon |

Keep away from the monsters in the last column, and keep a spare ring ready.

### Reading the Section 1a tables
Like Section 2, these tables grade the hunting grounds with the ring and list the 3 best for XP and the 3 best for gold per band. They only take hunting grounds where this lowers Prep and Hunt by 2 or more in total, or that Section 1 leaves out. The grades show the change, for example "2 → **0**". A dash ("– → 0") means the hunting ground isn't in Section 1 at all, because without the ring it's too annoying. "Can't see you" names the main monsters that will ignore you; "Still" names the strongest one that can still hit you.

The XP and loot numbers use the same killing speed as Section 1. Real results may be different: you can't gather groups, but you also never stop to heal. Loot is shown before paying for the rings.

## Section 1b: Hunting grounds behind quests
The other sections leave out every hunting ground that needs a quest first, because quests take time and many players find them a chore. Two don't count: the Targuna Quest, which everyone on the island does anyway, and starting The New Frontier Quest, which is only talking to Ongulf. This section lists those hunting grounds, graded like Section 1, with the same two lists per band. When Section 1a makes one of them easier, its note says so.

### Yalahar
Yalahar is worth its quests: the depot is in the middle of the city, and most of its hunting grounds are a short walk away, in the quarters around it. The Foreigner and Magician quarters are open from your first visit. The other quarters' gates open as you start the missions of the [In Service of Yalahar Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/In_Service_of_Yalahar_Quest): mission 3 opens the Alchemist Quarter, 4 the Trade Quarter, 5 the Arena and Zoo Quarter, 6 the Cemetery Quarter, 7 the Sunken Quarter and 8 the Factory Quarter. A gate opens as soon as its mission starts, so you don't have to finish that mission first. Getting there takes two short parts of the same quest: The Way to Yalahar for your first trip, and Searoutes around Yalahar for the boats back out. [The long game](progression.md) lists each step with its level.

### Access quests
The quests these hunting grounds need, in the order you'll first need them, so you can do each one while you're nearby. **Part needed** is how much of the quest opens the hunting ground: you rarely need the whole quest. The quest names link to TibiaWiki for the walkthrough. Each band's **Unlock first** line names the quests that band adds.

| Quest | Part needed: hunting grounds it opens | First needed | Premium | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [Barbarian Test Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Barbarian_Test_Quest) | needed before the Ice Islands Quest, TibiaWiki suggests level 25 for the quest: Nibelor<br>for Buddel's boat, TibiaWiki suggests level 25 for the quest: Jotunar, Tyrsung, Tyrsung Furrier Camp, Hrodmir Stone Golem Caves<br>for Buddel's boat, or walk over the Formorgar Glacier, TibiaWiki suggests level 25 for the quest: Ragnir barbarians, Bittermor barbarians, Krimhorn barbarians (surface), Barbarian Settlements, Krimhorn barbarians (+5 to +6) | 20–40 | yes | Svargrond |
| [The Ice Islands Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/The_Ice_Islands_Quest) | first mission: Nibelor<br>"Nibelor 2" mission started, TibiaWiki suggests level 130+ for the quest: Jotunar, Tyrsung, Tyrsung Furrier Camp | 20–40 | yes | Svargrond |
| [Treasure Island Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Treasure_Island_Quest) | for the caverns, TibiaWiki suggests level 40 for the quest: Treasure Island (surface), Treasure Island (-1 to -3) | 20–40 | yes | Treasure Island |
| [The Shattered Isles Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/The_Shattered_Isles_Quest) | Access to Nargor part, after Access to Meriana: five errands in Sabrehaven (100 Bread, a Whisper Beer, a letter, an Atlas, 5 Pirate Voodoo Dolls), then ask Duncan for a mission; no level needed: Nargor Undead Cave, Crystal Gardens<br>Access to Meriana part, no level needed: errands, a Rake, a Love Poem and about 1,000 gold; it also unlocks Marina's sea turtles: Laguna Islands, Blood Crabs<br>Access to Meriana part, no level needed: errands, a Rake, a Love Poem and about 1,000 gold: Meriana Gargoyle Cave | 60–80 | yes | Liberty Bay |
| [In Service of Yalahar Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/In_Service_of_Yalahar_Quest) | mission 5 started, which opens the Arena and Zoo Quarter gate: Arena and Zoo Quarter (-1 to -5)<br>mission 8 started, which opens the Factory Quarter gate: Factory Quarter (-1 to -2) | 100–150 | partly | Edron |
| [Blood Brothers Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Blood_Brothers_Quest) | mission 4, TibiaWiki suggests level 70+ for the quest: Vengoth | 200–250 | yes | Yalahar |
| [Lion's Rock Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Lion%27s_Rock_Quest) | it opens the portal to the hunting ground, TibiaWiki suggests level 70 for the quest: Lion's Rock (Darashia) | 200–250 | yes | Lion's Rock |
| [Forgotten Knowledge Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Forgotten_Knowledge_Quest) | Death Portal: after the 5-tome first part, give Albinius 50 Pelvis Bones; no level needed: Old Masonry (-1 to -2), Old Masonry (-3) | 300–350 | yes | Halls of Hope |

## Section 1c: Hunting grounds with a long trip
The other sections also leave out hunting grounds more than about 2 minutes from the depot, counting each boat or turtle ride as about 30 seconds: every trip to sell loot or restock costs that time twice. Many of them are good, so this section lists them, graded like Section 1, with the same two lists per band. The Depot column shows the trip. Hunting grounds that also need a quest are in Section 1b.

#### Level 20–40 with a long trip (~38 hours to the next band)

**Unlock first:** [The New Frontier Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/The_New_Frontier_Quest) (started: ask Ongulf for a mission, which opens the doors to the elevators, for Gnarlhound Caves).

**Good for XP**

| Hunting ground | Prep | Hunt | XP/h | Loot/h | Depot | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [Gnarlhound Caves](https://tibiamaps.io/map#33193,31473,7:2) ✓ | 0 | 0 | ~14k–17k | ~115k–135k | Edron, then 3–4+ min: boat to Cormaya, steamboat to Farmine, the elevator and several exani hur ledges | Monsters: [Gnarlhound](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Gnarlhound). **Tested** (a level 20–30 knight): 400 gold of supplies per hour, Shaggy Tail sold for about 2.2k, safe but slow kills, no other players there, 134k loot/h on that world, 14k XP/h. The Farmine depot opens only after mission 5 of The New Frontier Quest. Watch: Gnarlhound hits 19% of HP. Sells: Shaggy Tail |

**Good for gold**

| Hunting ground | Prep | Hunt | XP/h | Loot/h | Depot | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [Gnarlhound Caves](https://tibiamaps.io/map#33193,31473,7:2) ✓ | 0 | 0 | ~14k–17k | ~115k–135k | Edron, then 3–4+ min: boat to Cormaya, steamboat to Farmine, the elevator and several exani hur ledges | Monsters: [Gnarlhound](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Gnarlhound). **Tested** (a level 20–30 knight): 400 gold of supplies per hour, Shaggy Tail sold for about 2.2k, safe but slow kills, no other players there, 134k loot/h on that world, 14k XP/h. ## Section 2: Imbuements
An **imbuement** is an enchantment you add to a piece of gear for 20 hours of fighting. For a comfortable knight, two matter far more than any other: **Vampirism** (life leech) and **Void** (mana leech), which turn part of the damage you deal into health and mana. You drink fewer potions and can fight bigger groups safely. This section's kit is **Basic Vampirism on your armor and Basic Void on your helmet**.

Leech gives back a share of your damage, so it pays off only once you deal a lot of it, around level 30 to 50 for a knight. That's why the Section 2 tables start at level 50.

### Setting it up
1. **Unlock imbuing** with the first part of the [Forgotten Knowledge Quest](https://tibia.fandom.com/wiki/Forgotten_Knowledge_Quest/Spoiler): in the Halls of Hope, just north of Thais, give 5 Heavy Old Tomes to Albinius (say "temple", then "yes"). Tomes cost about 250 gold on the Market, and orcs, trolls, skeletons, ghouls and cyclops drop them.
2. **Armor:** at an Imbuing Shrine in any big city, add Basic Vampirism with 25 Vampire Teeth and a 7,500 gold fee.
3. **Helmet:** use a Basic Void scroll from the Market. Scrolls work anywhere, without the quest or a shrine, and for Void the scroll is the cheaper way.
4. **Weapon, from level 200:** a second Basic Void scroll. The two add up to 6% mana leech, enough for the spells without potions, and far cheaper than Intricate Void (5%).

The kit costs about 74k gold per 20 hours of fighting, around 3.7k per hour (about 130k and 6.5k from level 200, with the second Void). Costs, using Market prices from November 2026:

| Imbuement | Cheapest way, per 20 h | Per hour |
|---|---|---|
| Basic Vampirism | shrine ~19k | ~0.9k |
| Basic Void | scroll ~55k | ~2.8k |
| Basic Strike | scroll ~35k | ~1.7k |
| Basic Slash | shrine ~10k | ~0.5k |
| Intricate Vampirism | shrine ~284k | ~14.2k |
| Intricate Void | shrine ~265k | ~13.2k |

### Good to know
- The 20 hours count down only while you fight. Removing an imbuement costs 15,000 gold, so let it run out.
- An item has 0 to 3 slots and holds one imbuement of each kind. Legs can't be imbued.
- Worth less for the gold, so later: **Strike** (critical hits, +1% to +4.5% damage), **Slash, Bash or Chop** (sword, club or axe skill, about +0.7% damage per point), and **elemental protection** (3% to 15% less damage from one element).
- You can farm the materials: Vampire Teeth from vampires (Edron Vampire Crypt), Rope Belts for Void from cultists (Cult Cave, Liberty Bay), and Bloody Pincers for Intricate Vampirism from blood crabs (Laguna Islands).

### Reading the Section 2 tables
These tables grade the hunting grounds with the kit and list the 3 best for XP and the 3 best for gold per band. They only take hunting grounds where this lowers Prep and Hunt by 2 or more in total. The grades show the change, for example "2 → **0**". The XP and loot numbers are the Section 1 estimates, so treat them as a minimum: with leech you should do better. One test so far, at Corym Mines with a level 99 knight using both Basic imbuements and Shield Bash: almost no supplies, about 80% less healing, and 28% more kills than the same knight at level 91 without either.

Leech helps most against steady damage. It doesn't help against a monster that can take half your health in one hit, against monsters that call helpers or turn invisible, or against elemental damage, so those hunting grounds keep their grades.

## Section 3: Charms and prey
Charms and prey give you bonuses against specific monsters. They cost little or nothing once unlocked, but you have to choose and switch them as you change hunting grounds.

### Prey
Open it with Ctrl+Y (or right-click your character and choose "Prey System"). Each prey slot offers a list of 9 creatures. You pick one, and you get a bonus against that creature for 2 hours of hunting.
- **Slots:** 2, and a third one can be bought in the Store.
- **Bonuses:** each one has 10 steps. **Damage reduction** cuts the damage that creature does to you by 12% to 30%. **Bonus experience** gives 13% to 40% more experience from it. **Damage boost** (7% to 25%) and **improved loot** (13% to 40%) also exist.
- **New list:** free every 20 hours, or for 150 gold per level of your character.
- **Prey Wildcards** let you improve the bonus or pick the creature you want. You get 3 each week from the daily reward, and they're also sold in the Store.

The comfort setup uses both premium slots:
1. **Damage reduction** on the monster that hits you hardest.
2. **Bonus experience** (or improved loot, at a money spot) on the monster that gives you the most experience.

The tables below assume about 20% damage reduction and 25% more experience, which is what you'll usually have after a wildcard or two.

### Charms
Every creature has a **Bestiary** entry. Killing enough of that creature completes the entry in 3 stages and gives **Charm Points**, more for tougher creatures. You spend the points to unlock charms, and then place each charm on one creature with a completed entry; minor charms (below) need only the second stage. You can use 6 charms at once, and moving a charm to another creature costs 100 gold per level of your character.

The best charms for comfort:
- **Dodge** (240 points): a 5% chance to take no damage from that creature's attack (10% after an upgrade for 360 more). Put it on the monster that hurts you most.
- **Wound** (240 points): each of your attacks has a 5% chance to hit for 5% of the creature's maximum health, roughly 1–2% more damage. If the creature is weak to an element, the matching charm is better (Freeze, Zap, Enflame, Poison, Curse or Divine Wrath).
- **Minor charms**, paid with Minor Charm Echoes instead of points: Vampiric Embrace (more life leech, if your gear already leeches), Gut (6% more creature products), Bless (lose 6% less when that creature kills you) and Scavenge (a 60–120% better chance to skin or dust that creature, see Loot in Section 1). You get 50 echoes for unlocking a major charm and 100 and 200 more for its two upgrades, plus 100 at promotion; a minor charm costs 100 echoes.
- **Low Blow** and **Savage Blow** (800 points each) add critical hits. They only pay off once you have a Strike imbuement.

Charms change your average damage, not the worst hit, so they don't change the grades in the tables.

### Reading the Section 3 tables
These tables grade the Section 1 hunting grounds with the damage-reduction prey and list the 3 best for XP and the 3 best for gold per band. They only take hunting grounds where this lowers Prep and Hunt by 2 or more in total. The notes say which creature to pick for each prey slot. The XP numbers include the bonus-experience prey.

Damage-reduction prey on Giant Spider, XP prey on Tarantula. Still: summoners |

## Section 4: Wheel of Destiny and gems
From level 51, after promotion and with a premium account, you get **1 point per level** to spend on the Wheel of Destiny, a big circle of permanent perks: 40 points at level 90, 84 at 134 and 150 at 200. You can move your points around for free in any temple.

### How the Wheel works
- You start in the middle and fill **slices** with points. Each point gives a small **dedication** bonus. For a knight, the hit-point slices give 3 extra HP per point; other slices give mana, capacity or mitigation (each point reduces damage taken a little).
- A full slice unlocks a **conviction perk**. The ones near the middle are small but useful, such as extra mana leech. **Battle Healing** is the best for comfort (your healing spells heal 10% more, and three times that, 30%, while you wear a shield), but it sits on the outer ring, about 575 points away, so it's a goal for around level 600 and up. Others improve your spells: `exori gran` costs 30 less mana, `exura gran ico` heals 125% more, and so on.
- With 250 points in one quarter of the Wheel (around level 300 and up) you reach the big **revelation perks**, such as Combat Mastery (you take less damage the lower your health is, doubled with a shield).

A good comfort plan: fill the hit-point slices first, then the next slice out from them, which gives mana leech. TibiaPal has a planner and ready-made knight builds at tibiapal.com/wheels.

### Gems
Later, the Wheel also takes **gems**, which add extra bonuses such as more HP or elemental resistance. Lesser gems drop from influenced monsters (stronger versions of normal monsters that appear now and then), bigger ones from fiendish monsters and bosses. Revealing a gem's bonuses costs gold: 125k for a lesser gem, 1 million for a regular one and 6 million for a greater one. A gem only fits the slot of its own colour, and it stays off until you fill that colour's gem slices in the Wheel: 1 for a lesser gem, 2 for a regular one. Lesser and regular gems only add survival (resistance, HP, mana, capacity); greater gems add one special bonus, such as life leech, mana leech, critical damage or dodge, which suits this guide's play style best. That's late-game spending, so gems aren't in the tables.

### Reading the Section 4 tables
These tables assume half your points go into hit-point slices, about 1.5 extra HP per level above 50. They grade the Section 1 hunting grounds with that extra health and list the 3 best for XP and the 3 best for gold per band. They only take hunting grounds where this lowers Prep and Hunt by 2 or more in total. Battle Healing makes every heal stronger too, which the tables don't count.



## Section 5: Weapon proficiency
Every monster you kill with a weapon equipped builds up that weapon's **proficiency**. Each proficiency level unlocks a choice of perks for that weapon, such as more damage, more critical hits or more leech. You pick one perk per level. You can pick anywhere, but changing a perk needs a protection zone.

Progress belongs to each weapon, so switching weapons often wastes it. Pick one main weapon per level band and stay with it.

**How fast it grows.** Knight weapons need 1,250 progress for level 1, then 20,000, 80,000, 300,000, 1.5 million, 6 million and 20 million for level 7, the last level with perks. Each kill gives progress based on the creature's difficulty: 30 for trivial, 70 for easy, 100 for medium, 165 for hard and 240 for challenging creatures (influenced monsters give a bit more).

At a comfortable hunting ground you kill roughly 300 to 500 monsters an hour, mostly easy and medium ones, so about 25,000 to 50,000 progress per hour:

| Proficiency level | Hunting time with one weapon |
|---|---|
| 1 and 2 | under an hour |
| 3 | 2 to 3 hours |
| 4 | 6 to 12 hours |
| 5 | 30 to 60 hours |
| 6 | 120 to 240 hours |
| 7 | 400 hours or more |

So the first four levels come quickly with any weapon you keep for a band. Levels 5 and up are for a weapon you'll use for a long time.

Each weapon has its own perks, and from proficiency level 3 you can swap one of them for another perk (250 Dust). The weapon's TibiaWiki page and the planner at tibiapal.com/weapon-proficiency list them.

## Section 6: The Forge and the full Elite Knight
The last step is the **Exaltation Forge** in the Adventurers' Guild. It raises an item's **tier**, which adds a small chance-based effect:

| Item | Effect | Tier 1 | Tier 3 | Tier 10 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Weapon | Onslaught: chance of a hit with 60% extra damage | +0.3% damage | +1% damage | +5.4% damage |
| Armor | Ruse: chance to dodge an attack | 0.5% | 1.6% | 7.5% |
| Helmet | Momentum: chance to cut 2 seconds off your spell cooldowns | 2% every 2 s | 6% | 24% |
| Legs | Transcendence: chance to become an avatar for 7 seconds | very rare | rare | about 2% |
| Boots | Amplification: makes the other effects trigger more often | +2.5% | +9% | +57% |

**How it works.** You **fuse** two identical items of the same tier, paying gold and Dust, and get one item of the next tier. It works half the time (65% with an Exalted Core). When it fails, one of the two items loses a tier, or is destroyed if it was tier 0, and the other stays as it was; an Exalted Core makes that loss happen only half the time. So fusing your weapon with a second copy never costs more than the copy. Dust comes from influenced monsters and can't be traded; Exalted Cores are made from it.

**How far an item can go** depends on its classification, from 1 to 4. Class 1 items stop at tier 1, class 2 at tier 2, class 3 at tier 3, and class 4 items reach tier 10.

**What it costs.** Going from tier 0 to 1 costs 25k gold for a class 1 item, 750k for class 2, 4 million for class 3 and 8 million for class 4. The next tiers of a class 4 item cost 20 million, then 40 million, 65 million and more.

**Without risk.** Three Forge actions always work:
- **Transfer** moves a tier from one item to a tier 0 item of the same slot and classification, which gets the tier minus one; the item it comes from is destroyed. A tier 2 transfer (landing as tier 1) costs 100 Dust, 1 Exalted Core and 5 million gold for class 2, 10 million for class 3 or 20 million for class 4. Use it to carry your old weapon's tier to a new one, or to put the tier of a tiered item from the Market on the item you want.
- **Convergence Fusion** (class 4 only) fuses two different items of the same slot and tier, and always works: 130 Dust and 55 million gold for tier 1, against 8 million for a normal fusion.
- **Convergence Transfer** (class 4 only) moves a tier without losing one, even to another slot: 160 Dust and 65 million gold for tier 1.

The Forge also turns Dust into Slivers (60 Dust for 3) and Slivers into Exalted Cores (50 each), and raises how much Dust you can carry. Slivers and Exalted Cores sell on the Market, so Dust you don't need is gold. Items must have no imbuement to go into the Forge, so use it when one runs out.

**For comfort,** the effect that matters most is Ruse on your armor. At the tiers you can afford early (1 to 3), it's only a 0.5–1.6% dodge chance for millions of gold. It's often cheaper to buy an already-tiered item on the Market than to forge one, and to move a tier you already own, use Transfer, not Fusion. Leave the Forge for when everything else is done.

With imbuements, charms, prey, the Wheel and gems, weapon proficiency and tiered gear, you have what players call a **full setup**.

## Appendix: how the numbers are made
Nobody publishes hunting numbers for knights without a full setup, so the XP, loot and grades in the knight tables are calculated, then checked against real hunts. Here is how, in plain terms.

1. **You.** The typical knight of each band: its level, skill and the band's gear. Its attack and defense values follow TibiaWiki's formulas, which match the Cyclopedia of three real knights exactly. How hard its auto attacks, `exori` and `exori infir min` really hit comes from those knights' server logs. `exori ico scu` (Shield Bash, new in June 2026) has no published formula and no logged hit yet, so its damage is an estimate from its base power and the shield's defense.
2. **The monsters.** HP, experience and loot come from TibiaWiki; armor, weaknesses, healing, attacks and the helpers they call come from the Canary open-source server.
3. **The hunting ground.** Where each monster appears comes from Canary's game map: which ones come together in one pull, how far you walk between them, and how fast they come back. Only the part you can walk around counts (walkable tiles from tibiamaps.io): parts of a floor that you can only reach by going down and finding another way up are separate hunts, and the model takes the best one. Real hunts showed monsters coming back about half as fast as Canary's times, so the model uses that.
4. **One fight at a time.** For each pull: how long it takes to kill (your hits after their armor, their healing, `exori` once on a box and `exori gran` too from level 90, `exori ico scu` on your target from level 18, chasing the ones that keep their distance), how much they hurt you (after your armor, shield and protections) and how you heal (`exura ico` first, potions in the scary moments). Walking and looting fill the time between pulls.
5. **Putting it together.** Kills per hour, up to what the respawn allows, give XP/h and loot/h. Each drop is valued at whichever is higher: the shop price or the Market price (the middle price on three big worlds, November 2026); drops rarer than once every 3 hours are left out. The healing gives the grades, and the potions and mana give the supplies.

Because it works fight by fight, a hunting ground's numbers grow with your level the way they really do: harder hits, faster walking and more HP, until the hunting ground runs out of monsters.

**How far to trust them:**
- Against 17 real hunts (levels 19 to 135), the time per kill came out within about 15%. The model was tuned once on those hunts: fights take a little longer than the pure damage says, each kill costs about a second more, and low levels (below 50) a few seconds more. Those hunts were played with `exori infir min`, before `exori ico scu`; the tables now use `exori ico scu` from level 18, which gives about a fifth more XP and uses more mana.
- Supplies are a rough estimate.
- It doesn't know about other players in the same hunting ground, or about areas that change every day (Poacher Caves, Forsaken Mine).
- If a hunting ground's money comes from one single item (like Gloom Wolf Fur), hunting it a lot can push that item's Market price down.
- Targuna is newer than Canary's map, so its monsters and spawns come from another open-source server. Their HP, experience and armor match TibiaWiki, but their attacks hit more often than the tests showed, so Targuna's grades come from the tests.
- The few hunting grounds missing from both maps (like the Lost Dwarves in the Forsaken Mine) use the older, simpler estimate.

Use the numbers to compare hunting grounds with each other, not as promises.
